<a id="s001"></a>
#### #001 — Process Hollowing & Cobalt Strike Beacon Loader (`process-hollowing`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Malware & Process Execution (`MALWARE`) | **Code**: `T1055.012` | **Severity**: `CRITICAL` | **Priority**: Tier 1 (Flagship) | **Status**: `BENCHMARK_LIVE`
- **MITRE ATT&CK**: T1106, T1055.012, T1071.001 | **CVEs / Refs**: CVE-2023-36884, CVE-2024-21412 | **Threat Actors**: General / Multi-Actor | **Documented Memory Blocks**: Yes
- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) Microsoft Win32 API specifications define the exact CREATE_SUSPENDED (0x4), NtUnmapViewOfSection, VirtualAllocEx, and SetThreadContext (RCX) register mechanics; (2) Elastic Security Labs memory-hunting research explains how unbacked PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE (0x40) VAD regions and kernel EtwTiLogAllocVMRemote callbacks expose the hollowed image; and (3) Microsoft Sysinternals & MITRE ATT&CK document Sysmon EventID 25 (ProcessTampering: Image is replaced) and parent-process anomalies.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **Microsoft Win32 API Reference** — *CreateProcessW (CREATE_SUSPENDED), VirtualAllocEx & WriteProcessMemory* (`MSDN-WIN32-MEMORY`): https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/processthreadsapi/nf-processthreadsapi-createprocessw — Documents the Win32 virtual memory and thread context APIs modeled in stages 1–4.
  1. **Microsoft Sysinternals** — *Sysmon Event ID 25: ProcessTampering (Image is replaced)* (`SYSMON-EID-25`): https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sysinternals/downloads/sysmon#event-id-25-processtampering — Defines the telemetry schema when a process image is unmapped and replaced in user-mode memory.
  1. **Elastic Security Labs** — *Hunting Memory Injection & Process Tampering via Kernel ETW Threat Intelligence* (`ELASTIC-MEM-ETW`): https://www.elastic.co/security-labs — Provides Virtual Address Descriptor (VAD) private RWX inspection and EtwTiLogAllocVMRemote detection logic.

#### #002 — Fileless AMSI Memory Bypass & Reflective DLL Injection (`amsi-reflective-dll`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Malware & Process Execution (`MALWARE`) | **Code**: `T1562.001` | **Severity**: `CRITICAL` | **Priority**: Tier 1 (Flagship) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`

- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) Microsoft AMSI Architecture documentation defines how powershell.exe invokes amsi.dll!AmsiScanBuffer before script evaluation; (2) MDSec & MITRE T1562.001/T1620 research details the 6-byte VirtualProtect prologue patch (mov eax, 0x80070057; ret) and in-memory CLR [System.Reflection.Assembly]::Load($bytes) execution; and (3) Microsoft PowerShell Team & Defender TI document how EventID 4104 captures the initial bypass script block and how EtwTiLogProtectVM detects amsi.dll code-page modification.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **Microsoft Learn** — *Antimalware Scan Interface (AMSI) Developer Architecture* (`MS-AMSI-ARCH`): https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/amsi/antimalware-scan-interface-portal — Describes how amsi.dll and AmsiScanBuffer inspect PowerShell script blocks prior to execution.
  1. **Microsoft PowerShell Team** — *PowerShell Operational Log Event ID 4104 (Script Block Logging)* (`MS-PS-EID-4104`): https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.core/about/about_logging_windows — Documents de-obfuscated script block capture used to detect P/Invoke VirtualProtect calls.
  1. **The MITRE Corporation & Defender Threat Intelligence** — *MITRE ATT&CK T1620 & T1562.001: Reflective Code Loading & AMSI Memory Patching* (`ATT&CK-T1620-T1562`): https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1620/ — Details in-memory CLR assembly loading, Stephen Fewer ReflectiveLoader stubs, and ETW EtwTiLogProtectVM telemetry.

  1. **The MITRE Corporation & Ransomware DFIR Research** — *MITRE ATT&CK T1486 & T1490: Intermittent File Encryption & Recovery Inhibition* (`ATT&CK-T1486-T1490`): https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1486/ — Details partial/head-only file encryption mechanics (encrypting only the first 4 KB–64 KB per file) and WMI Win32_ShadowCopy deletion.

  1. **Microsoft Security Response Center (MSRC)** — *CVE-2024-21338 Windows Kernel Elevation of Privilege Vulnerability (appid.sys)* (`MSRC-CVE-2024-21338`): https://msrc.microsoft.com/update-guide/vulnerability/CVE-2024-21338 — Official Microsoft patch advisory for the AppLocker driver IOCTL pointer manipulation vulnerability.
  1. **Kaspersky GReAT & MITRE ATT&CK G0032** — *Lazarus Group (Diamond Sleet) KaolinRAT & FudModule Kernel Callback Blinding Analysis* (`KASPERSKY-LAZARUS-FUDMODULE`): https://attack.mitre.org/groups/G0032/ — Details how FudModule suspends PPL-protected EDR services and clears PsSetCreateProcessNotifyRoutine and ObRegisterCallbacks.

#### #012 — Microsoft Threat Intel — Forest Blizzard (APT28): Print Spooler CVE-2022-38028 & GooseEgg (`ms-forest-blizzard-gooseegg`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Malware & Process Execution (`MALWARE`) | **Code**: `CVE-2022-38028` | **Severity**: `HIGH` | **Priority**: Tier 2 (High-Impact) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`
- **MITRE ATT&CK**: T1068, T1547.012, T1546.015 | **CVEs / Refs**: CVE-2022-38028 | **Threat Actors**: General / Multi-Actor | **Documented Memory Blocks**: No
- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) Microsoft Threat Intelligence dissected Forest Blizzard’s (APT28) custom GooseEgg launcher (execute.bat + justice.exe) and the rogue wayzgoose.dll COM server; (2) Microsoft Security Response Center (MSRC CVE-2022-38028) documented the underlying Print Spooler vulnerability where MPDW-constraints.js inside the Windows DriverStore is modified to corrupt PrintFilterPipelineSvc.exe; and (3) ESET & MITRE G0007 research correlated APT28’s post-escalation SYSTEM credential harvesting and rundll32DllRegisterServer telemetry.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **Microsoft Threat Intelligence** — *Analyzing Forest Blizzard’s custom post-compromise tool for exploiting CVE-2022-38028 to obtain credentials* (`MSFT-GOOSEEGG-2024`): https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/security/blog/2024/04/22/analyzing-forest-blizzards-custom-post-compromise-tool-for-exploiting-cve-2022-38028-to-obtain-credentials/ — Documents execute.bat, justice.exe (GooseEgg), DriverStore pnms009.inf modification, and wayzgoose.dll SYSTEM execution inside spoolsv.exe.
  1. **Microsoft Security Response Center (MSRC)** — *CVE-2022-38028 Windows Print Spooler Elevation of Privilege Vulnerability* (`MSRC-CVE-2022-38028`): https://msrc.microsoft.com/update-guide/vulnerability/CVE-2022-38028 — Official vulnerability advisory for JavaScript Constraints file manipulation in Windows Print Spooler.
  1. **The MITRE Corporation & ESET Research** — *MITRE ATT&CK Group G0007: APT28 (Forest Blizzard / Fancy Bear / Fighting Ursa)* (`ATT&CK-G0007`): https://attack.mitre.org/groups/G0007/ — Maps APT28 local privilege escalation chains, PrintFilterPipelineSvc.exe child-process anomalies, and credential dumping.

#### #013 — Microsoft & Kaspersky — Salt Typhoon (GhostEmperor): Demodex Rootkit & Telecom Core Capture (`ms-salt-typhoon-telecom`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Malware & Process Execution (`MALWARE`) | **Code**: `T1014` | **Severity**: `CRITICAL` | **Priority**: Tier 3 (Specialized) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`
- **MITRE ATT&CK**: T1068, T1014, T1040 | **CVEs / Refs**: CVE-2023-20198 / CVE-2023-20273 | **Threat Actors**: General / Multi-Actor | **Documented Memory Blocks**: Yes
- **Redesign / Consolidation Note**: Overlap with #177 telecom-salt-typhoon — verify distinct angle or merge
- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) Joint CISA/NSA/FBI Guidance (AA24-338A) details how Salt Typhoon compromises carrier backbone routers, abuses Cisco IOS XE Guest Shell Linux containers, and captures TACACS+/RADIUS authentication keys; (2) Kaspersky GReAT reverse-engineered the GhostEmperor Demodex kernel rootkit (Nguv64.sys loaded via vulnerable signed driver BYOVD) that unlinks EPROCESS structures and hides registry keys on Windows mediation servers; and (3) Cisco Talos & Microsoft Threat Intelligence documented CVE-2023-20198 / CVE-2023-20273 IOS XE Web UI exploitation and the GhostSpider user-mode C2 framework.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **CISA / NSA / FBI / Microsoft Threat Intelligence** — *Enhanced Visibility and Hardening Guidance for Communications Infrastructure (Salt Typhoon / AA24-338A)* (`CISA-AA24-338A`): https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/cybersecurity-advisories/aa24-338a — Documents Salt Typhoon (GhostEmperor) telecom core router intrusion, Guest Shell abuse, and TACACS+ credential capture.
  1. **Kaspersky GReAT** — *GhostEmperor: From ProxyLogon to Kernel Mode with Demodex Rootkit* (`KASPERSKY-DEMODEX`): https://securelist.com/ghostemperor-from-proxylogon-to-kernel-mode/104407/ — Technical analysis of the Demodex kernel rootkit driver, BYOVD loader, and DKOM registry/process hiding.
  1. **Cisco Talos Intelligence Group** — *Active Exploitation of Cisco IOS XE Software Web Management User Interface (CVE-2023-20198 & CVE-2023-20273)* (`TALOS-IOSXE-2023`): https://blog.talosintelligence.com/ — Explains how unauthenticated attackers create privilege-15 local accounts and launch the Guest Shell container on IOS XE routers.

<a id="s014"></a>
#### #014 — NCSC-NL, Talos & Mandiant — China-Nexus APT: FortiOS SSL-VPN & COATHANGER Persistent RAT (`talos-fortinet-coathanger-apt`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Malware & Process Execution (`MALWARE`) | **Code**: `CVE-2024-21762` | **Severity**: `CRITICAL` | **Priority**: Tier 2 (High-Impact) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`
- **MITRE ATT&CK**: T1190, T1574.006, T1014 | **CVEs / Refs**: CVE-2024-21762, CVE-2022-42475 | **Threat Actors**: General / Multi-Actor | **Documented Memory Blocks**: Yes
- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) Dutch Military Intelligence (MIVD/AIVD) & NCSC-NL discovered and reverse-engineered the COATHANGER rootkit inside the Dutch Ministry of Defence—showing how /etc/ld.so.preload loads preload.so to hook libc readdir/open/stat and how patching the flatkc boot unpacker survives FortiOS firmware upgrades; (2) FortiGuard PSIRT (FG-IR-22-398 & FG-IR-24-015) documented the underlying sslvpnd heap/out-of-bounds write exploits (CVE-2022-42475 & CVE-2024-21762); and (3) Mandiant appliance-forensics research detailed monolithic /bin/init ROP chains and out-of-band NetFlow detection.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **NCSC-NL (Netherlands MIVD/AIVD)** — *COATHANGER: Chinese Cyber Espionage Malware Targeting FortiGate Appliances* (`NCSC-NL-COATHANGER-2024`): https://english.ncsc.nl/publications/publications/2024/february/6/mivd-and-aivd-alert-on-coathanger-malware — Documents FortiOS SSL-VPN exploitation, `/data2/` implant storage, `/etc/ld.so.preload` libc hooking, and `flatkc` / `/bin/smartctl` firmware-upgrade persistence.
  1. **CISA & Fortinet PSIRT** — *FG-IR-24-015 (CVE-2024-21762) & FG-IR-22-398 (CVE-2022-42475) FortiOS SSL-VPN RCE Advisories* (`FG-IR-24-015`): https://www.fortiguard.com/psirt/FG-IR-24-015 — Official Fortinet PSIRT advisories for heap overflow and chunked transfer encoding out-of-bounds write in sslvpnd.
  1. **The MITRE Corporation & Appliance Forensics Research** — *MITRE ATT&CK T1574.006: Dynamic Linker Hijacking (LD_PRELOAD & /etc/ld.so.preload Rootkits)* (`ATT&CK-T1574.006`): https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1574/006/ — Explains userland dynamic-linker rootkits on Linux-based network appliances and why offline flash inspection is required.

<a id="s015"></a>
#### #015 — Kaspersky GReAT & Microsoft — Windows CLFS.sys Kernel Zero-Day EoP & Nokoyawa Ransomware (`windows-clfs-ransomware-cve-2023-28252-nokoyawa`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Malware & Process Execution (`MALWARE`) | **Code**: `CVE-2023-28252` | **Severity**: `CRITICAL` | **Priority**: Tier 2 (High-Impact) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`
- **MITRE ATT&CK**: T1068, T1134.001, T1486 | **CVEs / Refs**: CVE-2023-28252 | **Threat Actors**: General / Multi-Actor | **Documented Memory Blocks**: Yes
- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) Kaspersky GReAT (Securelist) captured the in-the-wild CVE-2023-28252 zero-day exploit during Nokoyawa ransomware intrusions, dissecting the crafted Base Log File (.blf) CRC32 forgery and the out-of-bounds write in CLFS!CClfsBaseFilePersisted::WriteMetadataBlock; (2) Zscaler ThreatLabz & Fortra analyzed the fake CClfsContainer vtable pivot to CLFS!ClfsEarlierLsn and nt!SeSetAccessStateGenericMapping to overwrite the current process EPROCESS Token with PID 4 (SYSTEM); and (3) Microsoft MSRC & CISA KEV issued the April 2023 Patch Tuesday fix.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **Kaspersky GReAT (Securelist)** — *Nokoyawa ransomware attacks use new Windows zero-day vulnerability in CLFS (CVE-2023-28252)* (`SECURELIST-CVE-2023-28252`): https://securelist.com/nokoyawa-ransomware-attacks-use-zero-day/109483/ — Documents discovery of the CVE-2023-28252 zero-day, .blf offset corruption, and Nokoyawa C++ ransomware deployment.
  1. **Zscaler ThreatLabz** — *Technical Analysis of Windows CLFS Zero-Day Vulnerability CVE-2023-28252 & Nokoyawa Ransomware* (`ZSCALER-CVE-2023-28252`): https://www.zscaler.com/blogs/security-research/technical-analysis-windows-clfs-zero-day-vulnerability-cve-2023-28252 — Details CClfsBaseFilePersisted::ExtendMetadataBlock / WriteMetadataBlock, fake pContainer vtables, and ClfsEarlierLsn gadgets.
  1. **Microsoft MSRC & CISA KEV** — *Windows Common Log File System Driver Elevation of Privilege Vulnerability (CVE-2023-28252)* (`MSRC-CVE-2023-28252`): https://msrc.microsoft.com/update-guide/vulnerability/CVE-2023-28252 — Official Microsoft patch advisory and CISA Known Exploited Vulnerabilities mandate.

#### #016 — Andres Freund, Red Hat & Kaspersky — XZ Utils (liblzma) IFUNC sshd Supply Chain Backdoor (`xz-utils-cve-2024-3094-backdoor`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Malware & Process Execution (`MALWARE`) | **Code**: `CVE-2024-3094` | **Severity**: `CRITICAL` | **Priority**: Tier 1 (Flagship) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`
- **MITRE ATT&CK**: T1195.002, T1574.006, T1556 | **CVEs / Refs**: CVE-2024-3094 | **Threat Actors**: General / Multi-Actor | **Documented Memory Blocks**: Yes
- **Redesign / Consolidation Note**: Evaluate moving to Category 5 (SUPPLY_CHAIN_CICD) during rebuild
- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) Andres Freund’s original oss-security disclosure documented the ~500ms sshd CPU latency anomaly, the build-to-host.m4 macro extraction of liblzma_la-crc64-fast.o from bad-3-corrupt_lzma2.xz, and the transitive libsystemd.so.0 -> liblzma.so.5 load chain; (2) Kaspersky GReAT reverse-engineered the glibc IFUNC resolver (_get_cpuid), the _dl_audit_preinit GOT hook on RSA_public_decrypt, and the ChaCha20 + Ed448 public-key command verification; and (3) Red Hat & CISA CVE-2024-3094 advisories provided affected distribution matrices and YARA/hash detection rules.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **Andres Freund (Microsoft / OpenWall oss-security)** — *backdoor in upstream xz/liblzma leading to ssh server compromise (CVE-2024-3094)* (`OSS-SEC-2024-03-29`): https://www.openwall.com/lists/oss-security/2024/03/29/4 — Documents the perf CPU anomaly discovery, m4 build script extraction, and libsystemd -> liblzma -> sshd load path.
  1. **Kaspersky GReAT (Securelist)** — *CVE-2024-3094: Technical Analysis of the XZ Utils Backdoor & Ed448 Payload Structure* (`SECURELIST-XZ-2024`): https://securelist.com/xz-backdoor-story-part-1/112354/ — Reverse-engineers the IFUNC crc64_resolve hook, RSA_public_decrypt GOT redirection, and Ed448 signature check.
  1. **Red Hat Product Security & CISA** — *Urgent Security Alert for Fedora 40/Rawhide & CISA CVE-2024-3094 Supply Chain Advisory* (`CISA-CVE-2024-3094`): https://www.redhat.com/en/blog/urgent-security-alert-fedora-41-and-rawhide-users — Provides affected package versions (5.6.0/5.6.1), systemd linkage prerequisites, and remediation guidance.

- **MITRE ATT&CK**: T1098.004, T1556.003, T1021.004 | **CVEs / Refs**: CVE-2023-36884, CVE-2024-21412 | **Threat Actors**: General / Multi-Actor | **Documented Memory Blocks**: No

  1. **Elastic Security Labs** — *Hunting Memory Injection & Process Tampering via Kernel ETW Threat Intelligence* (`ELASTIC-MEM-ETW`): https://www.elastic.co/security-labs — Provides Virtual Address Descriptor (VAD) private RWX inspection and EtwTiLogAllocVMRemote detection logic.

<a id="s031"></a>
#### #031 — Active Directory Kerberoasting & Pass-the-Hash Lateral Movement (`ad-kerberoasting`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Active Directory & Identity (`ACTIVE_DIRECTORY`) | **Code**: `T1558.003` | **Severity**: `HIGH` | **Priority**: Tier 1 (Flagship) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`
- **MITRE ATT&CK**: T1558.003, T1550.002, T1543.003 | **CVEs / Refs**: CVE-2022-33679, CVE-2021-42287 / CVE-2021-42278 | **Threat Actors**: General / Multi-Actor | **Documented Memory Blocks**: No
- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) MITRE ATT&CK T1558.003 & SpecterOps Kerberos research explain LDAP SPN discovery and why requesting EncryptionType 0x17 (RC4-HMAC-MD5) forces the Domain Controller to encrypt the TGS ticket using the service account’s NTLM hash; (2) Microsoft Windows Security Auditing documentation specifies EventID 4769 (TicketOptions 0x40810000) and EventID 4624 (LogonType 3 NtLmSsp lateral movement); and (3) NSA/CISA Active Directory hardening guidance provides AES-only enforcement and Group Managed Service Account (gMSA) mitigations.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **Microsoft Windows Security Auditing** — *Event ID 4769: A Kerberos service ticket was requested (RC4 0x17)* (`MS-AUDITING-4769`): https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/it-pro/windows-10/security/threat-protection/auditing/event-4769 — Documents TicketEncryptionType 0x17 (RC4-HMAC) and TicketOptions 0x40810000 auditing on Domain Controllers.
  1. **Microsoft Windows Security Auditing** — *Event ID 4624: An account was successfully logged on (LogonType 3)* (`MS-AUDITING-4624`): https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/it-pro/windows-10/security/threat-protection/auditing/event-4624 — Defines NTLMv2 network logon telemetry used to detect lateral movement across SMB.
  1. **NSA & CISA Cybersecurity Technical Guidance** — *Mitigating Kerberoasting and Active Directory Service Account Compromise* (`NSA-CISA-AD-KERBEROS`): https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1558/003/ — Details offline Hashcat mode 13100 cracking risks, AES256-CTS-HMAC-SHA1-96 enforcement, and gMSA remediation.

#### #035 — Microsoft, Rapid7 & Elastic — Windows "PrintNightmare": RpcAddPrinterDriverEx Remote SYSTEM RCE (`printnightmare-spooler-cve-2021-34527`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Active Directory & Identity (`ACTIVE_DIRECTORY`) | **Code**: `CVE-2021-34527` | **Severity**: `CRITICAL` | **Priority**: Tier 1 (Flagship) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`
- **MITRE ATT&CK**: T1210, T1547.012, T1068 | **CVEs / Refs**: CVE-2021-34527 | **Threat Actors**: General / Multi-Actor | **Documented Memory Blocks**: Yes
- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) Microsoft MSRC CVE-2021-34527 & KB5005010 explain why RpcAddPrinterDriverEx() in localspl.dll allowed non-administrators to bypass ValidateObjectAccess() when passing flag 0x8014 (APD_INSTALL_WARNED_DRIVER \| APD_COPY_ALL_FILES \| APD_COPY_FROM_DIRECTORY); (2) Rapid7 & Sangfor researchers documented how pConfigFile pointing to an attacker SMB share (\\ip\share\evil.dll) forces spoolsv.exe to load and execute DllMain() as NT AUTHORITY\SYSTEM; and (3) Elastic Security Labs & CISA authored Sysmon EventID 7/11 rules for C:\Windows\System32\spool\drivers\x64\3\ and PrintService EventID 316.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **Microsoft Security Response Center (MSRC)** — *Windows Print Spooler Remote Code Execution Vulnerability (CVE-2021-34527 / PrintNightmare)* (`MSRC-CVE-2021-34527`): https://msrc.microsoft.com/update-guide/vulnerability/CVE-2021-34527 — Documents the RpcAddPrinterDriverEx authorization bypass and Point and Print RestrictDriverInstallationToAdministrators fix.
  1. **Rapid7 Emergent Threat Response** — *PrintNightmare (CVE-2021-34527): Technical Analysis of MS-RPRN RpcAddPrinterDriverEx Exploitation* (`RAPID7-PRINTNIGHTMARE-2021`): https://www.rapid7.com/blog/post/2021/06/30/printnightmare-cve-2021-1675-remote-code-execution-in-windows-spooler-service/ — Details the DRIVER_CONTAINER structure, dwFileCopyFlags 0x8014 bypass, and UNC pConfigFile DLL loading.
  1. **Elastic Security Labs & CISA** — *Detecting PrintNightmare via PrintService Operational EventID 316 and Sysmon EventID 7/11* (`CISA-PRINTNIGHTMARE-2021`): https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/alerts/2021/06/30/printnightmare-critical-windows-print-spooler-vulnerability — Provides detection signatures for unsigned DLL drops in spool\drivers\x64\3\New\ and spoolsv.exe child shells.

- **Sidebar Category**: Active Directory & Identity (`ACTIVE_DIRECTORY`) | **Code**: `T1078.004` | **Severity**: `CRITICAL` | **Priority**: Tier 2 (High-Impact) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`

- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **Palo Alto Networks Unit 42** — *Muddled Libra (Scattered Spider) Cloud Identity & VMware ESXi Intrusion Playbook* (`UNIT42-MUDDLED-LIBRA-2024`): https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/muddled-libra/ — Details helpdesk social engineering, rogue FIDO2 MFA registration, RMM deployment, and AWS/ESXi cloud pivot.
  1. **CISA & FBI Joint Advisory** — *Scattered Spider (AA23-320A) Cybersecurity Advisory* (`CISA-AA23-320A`): https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/cybersecurity-advisories/aa23-320a — Corroborates Muddled Libra / Scattered Spider cloud-to-hypervisor TTPs and RMM abuse.
  1. **Mandiant / Google Cloud Threat Intelligence** — *UNC3944: SMS Phishing, SIM Swapping, and Cloud Identity Provider Intrusions* (`MANDIANT-UNC3944`): https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/threat-intelligence/ — Documents internal SharePoint/Confluence runbook reconnaissance, telecom BPO pivoting, and SSO federation persistence.

<a id="s037"></a>
#### #037 — Secura, CISA & CrowdStrike — Active Directory "Zerologon": Netlogon AES-CFB8 All-Zero IV Takeover (`zerologon-netlogon-cve-2020-1472`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Active Directory & Identity (`ACTIVE_DIRECTORY`) | **Code**: `CVE-2020-1472` | **Severity**: `CRITICAL` | **Priority**: Tier 1 (Flagship) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`
- **MITRE ATT&CK**: T1210, T1098, T1003.006 | **CVEs / Refs**: CVE-2020-1472 | **Threat Actors**: General / Multi-Actor | **Documented Memory Blocks**: Yes
- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) Secura (Tom Tervoort) published the mathematical proof of CVE-2020-1472 showing why ComputeNetlogonCredential’s hardcoded 16-byte all-zero AES-CFB8 IV causes 1 in 256 all-zero plaintexts to encrypt to 8 zero bytes; (2) CISA Emergency Directive 20-04 documented NetrServerPasswordSet2 resetting the Domain Controller’s AD password hash to empty (31d6cfe0d16ae931b73c59d7e0c089c0) and breaking DNS/replication until restored from HKLM\SECURITY\Policy\Secrets\$MACHINE.ACC; and (3) CrowdStrike & Microsoft documented EventID 4742 (ANONYMOUS LOGON) and EventID 5805 storms.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **Secura BV (Tom Tervoort)** — *Zerologon: Unauthenticated Domain Controller Compromise by Subverting Netlogon Cryptography (CVE-2020-1472)* (`SECURA-ZEROLOGON-2020`): https://www.secura.com/blog/zero-logon — Explains the MS-NRPC AES-CFB8 all-zero IV flaw, the 256-attempt brute force, and signing/sealing flag bypass.
  1. **Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency (CISA)** — *Emergency Directive 20-04: Mitigate Netlogon Elevation of Privilege Vulnerability from August 2020 Patch Tuesday* (`CISA-ED-20-04`): https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/directives/ed-20-04-mitigate-netlogon-elevation-privilege-vulnerability-august-2020-patch — Mandates Secure RPC enforcement and documents DC machine password recovery after NetrServerPasswordSet2.
  1. **Microsoft Threat Intelligence & CrowdStrike** — *Detecting CVE-2020-1472 (Zerologon) via Windows EventID 5805, 4742 & Netlogon RPC Inspection* (`MS-DEFENDER-ZEROLOGON`): https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/security/blog/2020/09/28/new-detections-for-cve-2020-1472-active-exploitation/ — Provides EventID 4742 (ANONYMOUS LOGON modifying DC account) and System EventID 5805 detection rules.

#### #048 — DNS Tunneling C2 & High-Entropy Subdomain Data Exfiltration (`dns-tunneling-exfil`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Network & C2 Exfiltration (`NETWORK_WEB`) | **Code**: `T1071.004` | **Severity**: `HIGH` | **Priority**: Tier 1 (Flagship) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`

- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) IETF RFC 1035 defines the 63-byte subdomain label and 253-byte FQDN protocol limits that dictate how data chunks are segmented into DNS queries; (2) Palo Alto Networks Unit 42 & SANS DFIR research on APT34 (OilRig) DNS tunneling explains Base32/Hex encoding, cache-busting sequence prefixes, and Shannon entropy (>4.2 bits/byte) detection; and (3) Microsoft Sysinternals Sysmon EventID 22 maps each anomalous DNS query back to the exact originating process PID on the host.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **IETF Network Working Group** — *RFC 1035: Domain Names — Implementation and Specification* (`IETF-RFC-1035`): https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc1035 — Defines DNS TXT/CNAME record structures and subdomain label length constraints.

  1. **Palo Alto Networks Unit 42** — *DNS Tunneling: How DNS Can Be Abused by Malicious Actors (OilRig / APT34 Tradecraft)* (`UNIT42-DNS-TUNNELING`): https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/dns-tunneling-how-dns-can-be-abused-by-malicious-actors/ — Details Base32/Base64 subdomain payload chunking, TXT record C2 tasking, and recursive resolver inspection.

- **MITRE ATT&CK**: T1190, T1574.007, T1059.004 | **CVEs / Refs**: CVE-2023-36845, CVE-2023-36846 | **Threat Actors**: General / Multi-Actor | **Documented Memory Blocks**: Yes
- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) watchTowr Labs originally chained CVE-2023-36845 (Appweb CGI environment variable injection) with CVE-2023-36846 (do_upload uploaded file path); (2) VulnCheck then demonstrated how an attacker can achieve 100% fileless pre-auth RCE using CVE-2023-36845 alone by sending POST /webauth_operation.php?PHPRC=/dev/fd/0 with body auto_prepend_file="data://text/plain;base64,..." so php-cgi parses standard input as php.ini and executes in-memory PHP code without touching disk; and (3) Juniper SIRT & CISA KEV documented in-the-wild exploitation across SRX and EX devices.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **watchTowr Labs** — *CVEs-2023-36844 to 36847: Juniper SRX & EX Pre-Auth Remote Code Execution Chain* (`WATCHTOWR-JUNIPER-2023`): https://labs.watchtowr.com/cve-2023-36844-and-friends-rce-in-juniper-firewalls/ — Discovers how Appweb CGI populates process environment variables from query parameters (PHPRC).
  1. **VulnCheck Initial Access Intelligence** — *Single-Request Fileless RCE on Juniper J-Web via PHPRC=/dev/fd/0 (CVE-2023-36845)* (`VULNCHECK-CVE-2023-36845`): https://vulncheck.com/blog/juniper-cve-2023-36845 — Demonstrates single-request fileless RCE using PHPRC=/dev/fd/0 and auto_prepend_file="data://text/plain;base64,...".
  1. **Juniper SIRT & CISA KEV** — *2023-08 Out-of-Cycle Security Bulletin: Junos OS SRX and EX Series J-Web Vulnerabilities (JSA72300)* (`JSA72300`): https://supportportal.juniper.net/s/article/2023-08-Out-of-Cycle-Security-Bulletin-Junos-OS-SRX-Series-and-EX-Series-Multiple-vulnerabilities-in-J-Web-can-be-combined-to-allow-a-preAuth-Remote-Code-Execution — Official Juniper advisory detailing J-Web remediation and delete system services web-management mitigation.

<a id="s057"></a>
#### #057 — Mandiant & CISA — APT29 (Midnight Blizzard): JetBrains TeamCity RCE & GraphicalProton (`mandiant-apt29-teamcity-jetstream`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Network & C2 Exfiltration (`NETWORK_WEB`) | **Code**: `CVE-2023-42793` | **Severity**: `CRITICAL` | **Priority**: Tier 2 (High-Impact) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`

- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) SonarSource & Rapid7 AttackerKB discovered and dissected the exact Java RequestInterceptors wildcard path-matching bug—explaining why POST /app/rest/users/id:1/tokens/RPC2 (ending in /RPC2) tricks TeamCity into treating an admin REST token-creation call as an unauthenticated XML-RPC endpoint; (2) Joint CISA/FBI/NSA/NCSC-UK/Mandiant Advisory AA23-347A documented how APT29 (Midnight Blizzard) abused /app/rest/debug/processes to spawn shells, harvest CI/CD secrets, and deploy the GraphicalProton backdoor (OneDrive/Dropbox C2); and (3) JetBrains Security & NVD provided the teamcity-javaLogging and audit log detection rules.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **CISA, FBI, NSA, NCSC-UK & Mandiant** — *Russian Foreign Intelligence Service (SVR / APT29) Exploiting JetBrains TeamCity CVE-2023-42793 (AA23-347A)* (`CISA-AA23-347A`): https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/cybersecurity-advisories/aa23-347a — Documents APT29 mass exploitation, `/app/rest/debug/processes` command execution, and GraphicalProton C2 deployment.
  1. **SonarSource & Rapid7 Vulnerability Research** — *JetBrains TeamCity Critical Auth Bypass (CVE-2023-42793): RequestInterceptors /**/RPC2 Root-Cause Analysis* (`SONAR-CVE-2023-42793`): https://www.sonarsource.com/blog/jetbrains-teamcity-averted-supply-chain-attack/ — Explains why the `/**/RPC2` wildcard in TeamCity RequestInterceptors bypassed authentication on `/app/rest/users/id:1/tokens/RPC2` and how `internal.debug.processes.enable=true` unlocks RCE.
  1. **NIST NVD & JetBrains Security** — *CVE-2023-42793 JetBrains TeamCity On-Premises Authentication Bypass & RCE* (`CVE-2023-42793`): https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2023-42793 — Official vulnerability advisory and `teamcity-server.log` / `teamcity-activities.log` token-creation forensic indicators.

<a id="s058"></a>
#### #058 — Qualys TRU & CISA — OpenSSH "regreSSHion": SIGALRM Async-Signal-Unsafe syslog() Heap RCE (`openssh-regreSSHion-cve-2024-6387`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Network & C2 Exfiltration (`NETWORK_WEB`) | **Code**: `CVE-2024-6387` | **Severity**: `CRITICAL` | **Priority**: Tier 1 (Flagship) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`
- **MITRE ATT&CK**: T1190, T1068, T1059.004 | **CVEs / Refs**: CVE-2024-6387 | **Threat Actors**: General / Multi-Actor | **Documented Memory Blocks**: Yes
- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) Qualys Threat Research Unit (TRU) discovered and weaponized CVE-2024-6387 ("regreSSHion"), demonstrating how interrupting a main-thread malloc()/free() inside sshd when LoginGraceTime expires triggers an async-signal-unsafe syslog() call inside grace_alarm_handler(), corrupting glibc ptmalloc2 arena chunks and forging a fake _IO_FILE vtable; (2) OpenSSH 9.8 Release Notes explained how commit ac211206 inadvertently removed #ifndef DO_LOG_SAFE_IN_SIGHAND from sigdie(), re-introducing CVE-2006-5051; and (3) CISA & Rapid7 provided network connection-flooding detection rules ("Timeout before authentication" storms).
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **Qualys Threat Research Unit (TRU)** — *regreSSHion: RCE in OpenSSH’s server, on glibc-based Linux systems (CVE-2024-6387)* (`QUALYS-CVE-2024-6387`): https://www.qualys.com/2024/07/01/cve-2024-6387/regresshion.txt — Details the SIGALRM race condition, ptmalloc2 heap feng shui, and _IO_FILE vtable code execution.
  1. **The OpenSSH Project** — *OpenSSH 9.8 Security Advisory: Async-Signal-Unsafe sigdie() Regression* (`OPENSSH-9.8-ADV`): https://www.openssh.com/txt/release-9.8 — Explains the October 2020 regression of CVE-2006-5051 and the LoginGraceTime=0 mitigation.
  1. **CISA & Rapid7 Emergent Threat Response** — *CVE-2024-6387 OpenSSH regreSSHion Detection & Mitigation Guidance* (`RAPID7-CVE-2024-6387`): https://www.rapid7.com/blog/post/2024/07/01/etr-openssh-remote-unauthenticated-code-execution-cve-2024-6387-regresshion/ — Provides SSH connection rate and auth.log "Timeout before authentication" detection heuristics.

<a id="s062"></a>
#### #062 — Splunk STRT, Mandiant & CISA — Citrix Bleed: NetScaler OOB Memory Leak & NSC_AAAC Hijack (`splunk-citrix-bleed-session-hijack`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Network & C2 Exfiltration (`NETWORK_WEB`) | **Code**: `CVE-2023-4966` | **Severity**: `CRITICAL` | **Priority**: Tier 1 (Flagship) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`
- **MITRE ATT&CK**: T1190, T1539, T1550.004 | **CVEs / Refs**: CVE-2023-4966 | **Threat Actors**: General / Multi-Actor | **Documented Memory Blocks**: No
- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) Assetnote Security Research reverse-engineered the NetScaler binary to uncover the exact C bug—how sending a ~24,576-byte Host header to /oauth/idp/.well-known/openid-configuration causes snprintf() (which returns the theoretical ~147 KB length rather than the 131 KB buffer cap) to over-read ~16 KB of adjacent heap RAM containing 65-character NSC_AAAC session tokens; (2) CISA/FBI/MS-ISAC & Mandiant (AA23-325A) documented LockBit 3.0 post-MFA session hijacking and why admins must execute kill aaa session -all / kill pcon -all after patching; and (3) Splunk SURGe authored the SIEM SPL rules detecting a single NSC_AAAC cookie used from multiple autonomous system numbers (ASNs).
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **Assetnote Security Research** — *Citrix Bleed: Leaking Session Tokens with CVE-2023-4966 (snprintf Heap Over-Read Root Cause)* (`ASSETNOTE-CVE2023-4966`): https://www.assetnote.io/resources/research/citrix-bleed-leaking-session-tokens-with-cve-2023-4966 — Proves how NetScaler inserts the HTTP Host header 6 times into a 131 KB buffer via snprintf() and passes the unchecked return value to the HTTP response writer.
  1. **Splunk SURGe Security Research** — *Citrix Bleed (CVE-2023-4966) Vulnerability Analysis and Detection Guidance* (`SPLUNK-SURGE-CVE2023-4966`): https://www.splunk.com/en_us/blog/security/citrix-bleed-cve-2023-4966-vulnerability-analysis-and-detection.html — Documents the `/oauth/idp/.well-known/openid-configuration` anomalous response size (>131 KB) and SPL session-hijack detection queries.
  1. **CISA / FBI / MS-ISAC / ASD Joint Advisory** — *Threat Actors Exploit Citrix Bleed (CVE-2023-4966) and Deploy LockBit 3.0 (AA23-325A)* (`CISA-AA23-325A`): https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/cybersecurity-advisories/aa23-325a — Details post-exploitation `kill aaa session -all` / `kill pcon -all` mitigation and DaaS VDI session hijacking.

- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) Volexity Threat Research discovered the zero-day campaign (UTA0218), capturing the initial firewall compromise, outbound curling of bash stage-1 scripts, running-config.xml theft, and internal SMB/winexe lateral movement; (2) Palo Alto Networks Unit 42 reverse-engineered the UPSTYLE Python backdoor injected into /usr/lib/python3.6/site-packages/system.pth, its inotify monitor on sslvpn_ngx_error.log, and its 6-hour cleanup thread; and (3) Rapid7 AttackerKB & PAN PSIRT documented how the SESSID cookie path traversal injects backtick shell commands into the root sendTelemetry cron job.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **Palo Alto Networks Unit 42** — *Threat Brief: Operation MidnightEclipse, Post-Exploitation Activity Related to CVE-2024-3400* (`UNIT42-MIDNIGHTECLIPSE-2024`): https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/cve-2024-3400/ — Documents the SESSID cookie path traversal, sendTelemetry cron execution, UPSTYLE Python backdoor in system.pth, and running-config.xml extraction.
  1. **Volexity Threat Research** — *Zero-Day Exploitation of Unauthenticated Remote Code Execution Vulnerability in GlobalProtect (CVE-2024-3400)* (`VOLEXITY-UTA0218-2024`): https://www.volexity.com/blog/2024/04/12/zero-day-exploitation-of-unauthenticated-remote-code-execution-vulnerability-in-globalprotect-cve-2024-3400/ — Details the initial zero-day discovery (UTA0218), telemetry file-creation probes, DPAPI/Active Directory pivoting, and network egress indicators.
  1. **Palo Alto Networks PSIRT & Rapid7 AttackerKB** — *CVE-2024-3400 PAN-OS: Arbitrary File Creation Leads to OS Command Injection in GlobalProtect* (`PAN-SA-2024-0005`): https://security.paloaltonetworks.com/CVE-2024-3400 — Official vendor advisory and exploit root-cause analysis detailing affected PAN-OS versions and telemetry remediations.

<a id="s067"></a>
#### #067 — Microsoft, Unit 42 & CISA — Star Blizzard / Storm-0539: Evilginx AiTM Cookie Theft (`unit42-star-blizzard-evilginx-aitm`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Network & C2 Exfiltration (`NETWORK_WEB`) | **Code**: `T1539` | **Severity**: `HIGH` | **Priority**: Tier 1 (Flagship) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`
- **MITRE ATT&CK**: T1539, T1550.004, T1564.008 | **CVEs / Refs**: MITRE-T1539 / T1557 | **Threat Actors**: General / Multi-Actor | **Documented Memory Blocks**: No
- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) Joint CISA/NCSC-UK/NSA/FBI Advisory AA23-341A documents Star Blizzard’s (SEABORGIUM / COLDRIVER) spear-phishing lures, protonmail reconnaissance, and cloud-hosted PDF redirects; (2) Microsoft Threat Intelligence dissects how Evilginx Adversary-in-the-Middle (AiTM) reverse proxies relay live Entra ID MFA challenges, capture the resulting ESTSAUTHPERSISTENT cookie, and create hidden New-InboxRule forwarding rules in Exchange Online; and (3) Palo Alto Networks Unit 42 & MITRE T1539 detail FIDO2/WebAuthn token-binding mitigations and impossible-travel SessionId correlation.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **CISA, NCSC-UK, NSA & FBI Joint Advisory** — *Star Blizzard (SEABORGIUM / COLDRIVER) Adversary-in-the-Middle Spear-Phishing & Session Cookie Theft (AA23-341A)* (`CISA-AA23-341A`): https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/cybersecurity-advisories/aa23-341a — Documents Star Blizzard spear-phishing infrastructure, Evilginx reverse-proxy phishlets, and target reconnaissance.
  1. **Microsoft Threat Intelligence** — *From Cookie Theft to BEC: Attackers Use AiTM Phishing Sites as Entry Point* (`MSFT-AITM-2022`): https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/security/blog/2022/07/12/from-cookie-theft-to-bec-attackers-use-aitm-phishing-sites-as-entry-point-to-further-financial-fraud/ — Explains real-time TLS reverse-proxy relaying of login.microsoftonline.com, ESTSAUTHPERSISTENT capture, and Exchange New-InboxRule persistence.
  1. **The MITRE Corporation & Palo Alto Unit 42** — *MITRE ATT&CK T1539: Steal Web Session Cookie & FIDO2 Token Binding Mitigations* (`ATT&CK-T1539`): https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/ — Defines adversary-in-the-middle session cookie theft and FIDO2/CAE token protection mitigations.

<a id="s068"></a>
#### #068 — Mandiant, VMware & CISA — UNC3886: vCenter vmdird Out-of-Bounds Write & ESXi VIRTUALPITA Backdoor (`vmware-vcenter-cve-2023-34048-unc3886`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Network & C2 Exfiltration (`NETWORK_WEB`) | **Code**: `CVE-2023-34048` | **Severity**: `CRITICAL` | **Priority**: Tier 2 (High-Impact) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`
- **MITRE ATT&CK**: T1190, T1552.001, T1547 | **CVEs / Refs**: CVE-2023-34048 | **Threat Actors**: General / Multi-Actor | **Documented Memory Blocks**: Yes
- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) Mandiant (Google Cloud) uncovered UNC3886’s multi-year zero-day exploitation of CVE-2023-34048 against VMware vCenter’s vmdird DCERPC service, their decryption of vpxuser credentials from vPostgreSQL using /etc/vmware-vpx/symkey.dat, and their deployment of custom ESXi VIB backdoors (VIRTUALPITA and VIRTUALPIE) communicating over VMCI / AF_VSOCK sockets; (2) VMware VMSA-2023-0023 documented the out-of-bounds write in vmdird on TCP port 2012; and (3) CISA KEV provided hypervisor VIB integrity auditing guidance.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **Mandiant Threat Intelligence (Google Cloud)** — *Cloaked and Covert: Uncovering UNC3886 Espionage Operations on VMware vCenter & ESXi (CVE-2023-34048)* (`MANDIANT-UNC3886-2024`): https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/threat-intelligence/uncovering-unc3886-espionage-operations — Documents vmdird core dump forensics, vpxuser decryption via symkey.dat, and VIRTUALPITA/VIRTUALPIE AF_VSOCK implants.
  1. **VMware / Broadcom Security Response** — *VMSA-2023-0023: VMware vCenter Server Out-of-Bounds Write Vulnerability (CVE-2023-34048)* (`VMSA-2023-0023`): https://support.broadcom.com/web/ecx/support-content-notification/-/external/content/SecurityAdvisories/0/23254 — Details the DCERPC protocol implementation flaw in vmdird and patched vCenter builds.
  1. **Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency (CISA)** — *CISA Adds VMware vCenter CVE-2023-34048 to Known Exploited Vulnerabilities Catalog* (`CISA-KEV-2023-34048`): https://www.cisa.gov/known-exploited-vulnerabilities-catalog — Mandates patching of vCenter DCERPC endpoints and auditing of ESXi VIB acceptance levels.

<a id="s069"></a>
#### #069 — TRAPA Security, Rapid7 & CISA — Zyxel Firewall Pre-Auth IKEv2 Packet Decoder Command Injection (`zyxel-firewall-cve-2023-28771-ike-rce`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Network & C2 Exfiltration (`NETWORK_WEB`) | **Code**: `CVE-2023-28771` | **Severity**: `CRITICAL` | **Priority**: Tier 2 (High-Impact) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`
- **MITRE ATT&CK**: T1190, T1059.004, T1583.005 | **CVEs / Refs**: CVE-2023-28771 | **Threat Actors**: General / Multi-Actor | **Documented Memory Blocks**: Yes
- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) TRAPA Security discovered CVE-2023-28771 in Zyxel ZyWALL/ATP/USG FLEX firewalls, reverse-engineering the sshipsecpm IKE daemon on UDP port 500 to recover the hardcoded 24-byte 3DES-CBC key inside ike_decoder_notification() (Notify Message Type 14) and the unsanitized system("echo ... >> /tmp/sdwan_vpndebug.log") call; (2) Rapid7 published the weaponized packet structure and Suricata UDP/500 rules; and (3) CISA KEV & Shadowserver tracked mass in-the-wild Mirai and ransomware exploitation.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **TRAPA Security Research** — *Root Cause Analysis of Zyxel Firewall Unauthenticated Remote Command Execution (CVE-2023-28771)* (`TRAPA-CVE-2023-28771`): https://trapa.security/blog/cve-2023-28771/ — Reverse-engineers sshipsecpm ike_decoder_notification(), the hardcoded 3DES key, and the sdwan_vpndebug.log command injection.
  1. **Rapid7 Emergent Threat Response** — *Zyxel Firewall Pre-Auth Command Injection (CVE-2023-28771) Exploit & Network Signatures* (`RAPID7-CVE-2023-28771`): https://attackerkb.com/topics/N3i8dxpFKS/cve-2023-28771/rapid7-analysis — Documents the IKEv2 SA_INIT Notify Type 14 packet layout ("HAXBHAXBHAXB") and Suricata detection rules.
  1. **Zyxel PSIRT & CISA KEV** — *Zyxel Security Advisory for OS Command Injection Vulnerability in Firewalls (CVE-2023-28771)* (`ZYXEL-SI-1486`): https://www.zyxel.com/global/en/support/security-advisories/zyxel-security-advisory-for-remote-command-injection-vulnerability-of-firewalls — Lists affected ATP, USG FLEX, VPN, and ZyWALL firmware versions and patches.

- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) Microsoft Win32 API specifications define the exact CREATE_SUSPENDED (0x4), NtUnmapViewOfSection, VirtualAllocEx, and SetThreadContext (RCX) register mechanics; (2) Elastic Security Labs memory-hunting research explains how unbacked PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE (0x40) VAD regions and kernel EtwTiLogAllocVMRemote callbacks expose the hollowed image; and (3) Microsoft Sysinternals & MITRE ATT&CK document Sysmon EventID 25 (ProcessTampering: Image is replaced) and parent-process anomalies.

  1. **Microsoft Win32 API Reference** — *CreateProcessW (CREATE_SUSPENDED), VirtualAllocEx & WriteProcessMemory* (`MSDN-WIN32-MEMORY`): https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/processthreadsapi/nf-processthreadsapi-createprocessw — Documents the Win32 virtual memory and thread context APIs modeled in stages 1–4.

#### #123 — VulnCheck — Control Web Panel (CWP) Pre-Auth Module Dispatch to Root RCE (CVE-2026-73695..104080) (`vulncheck-cwp-preauth-module-dispatch-rce`)

- **Sidebar Category**: Zero-Day Appliances (`ZERO_DAY_APPLIANCES`) | **Code**: `T1190` | **Severity**: `CRITICAL` | **Priority**: Tier 2 (High-Impact) | **Status**: `PENDING_REBUILD`
- **MITRE ATT&CK**: T1190, T1059.004, T1068, T1552.001 | **CVEs / Refs**: CVE-2026-73696 & CVE-2026-73695, CVE-2026-73697 & CVE-2026-77876, CVE-2026-77877, CVE-2026-77878 & CVE-2026-104080, CVE-2026-77879, CVE-2026-77880 & CVE-2026-77881 | **Threat Actors**: General / Multi-Actor | **Documented Memory Blocks**: No
- **Multi-Source Synthesis Reference**: Synthesizes 3 complementary sources: (1) VulnCheck Initial Access Intelligence ("Control Web Panel: Pre-Auth Module Dispatch to Root RCE", Nov 3, 2026) discovered and disclosed the 10-CVE chain (CVE-2026-73695..CVE-2026-104080), reverse-engineering the IonCube-encoded PHP builds (via icunpack across patches 1.8–1.30) to document how the missing exit() in UnauthenticatedFlowTrait::toLoginWithIntendedUri() (CVE-2026-73696) and unvalidated module include in services/users/index.php (CVE-2026-73695) expose phpini_editor.php (CVE-2026-73697) and the root-privileged loopback User API on 127.0.0.1:2302 (CVE-2026-77877..CVE-2026-104080); (2) Control Web Panel (CWP) ChangeLog & Security Patch Notes (0.9.8.1210–0.9.8.1213+) verify the remediation timeline; and (3) MITRE CVE / CWE & CISA KEV standards contextualize prior CWP exploitation (CVE-2025-48703) and Linux auditd detection.
- **Preserved Primary Sources (3)**:
  1. **VulnCheck Initial Access Intelligence (CNA)** — *Control Web Panel: Pre-Auth Module Dispatch to Root RCE (CVE-2026-73695..CVE-2026-104080)* (`VULNCHECK-CWP2SHELL-2026`): https://www.vulncheck.com/blog/control-web-panel-pre-auth-module-dispatch-rce — Primary technical disclosure detailing the 4-port CWP architecture (2031, 2087, 2083, 127.0.0.1:2302), all 10 CVEs, the GET /<account>/ 302 oracle, quote-free webshell split in phpini_editor.php, loopback root escalation in /v1/restoredatabase/ and /v1/newpass/, and icunpack patch diffs (1.8–1.30).
  1. **Control Web Panel (CentOS Web Panel)** — *CWP7 ChangeLog & Security Updates (Builds 0.9.8.1210 to 0.9.8.1213+ / Patches 1.12–1.30)* (`CWP7-PATCH-1.12-1.30`): https://control-webpanel.com/changelog — Documents the vendor fixes: adding exit() to toLoginWithIntendedUri(), basename/realpath module validation, get_user_safe_path(), prepared statements in access_token(), and cwp_api_as_user() privilege dropping.
  1. **NIST NVD & CISA Known Exploited Vulnerabilities (KEV)** — *Control Web Panel Historical Pre-Auth RCE Context (CVE-2025-48703 & CWE-698 Execution After Redirect)* (`CVE-2025-48703 / CWE-698`): https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-48703 — Provides historical baseline showing why CWP August 2026 patch for CVE-2025-48703 (fixing only filemanager) left the underlying CWE-698 EAR and module dispatcher exposed across all other 170+ user modules.
